          Assessment: A fresh executor has no stated D2 binding/activation allocation: real-bound fixture prerequisite tests see missing future artifacts and violate A05, while leaving them mock-bound requires an explicit later same-case real binding contract. The plan cannot rely on stale host artifacts or an undeclared filter to bridge this phase dependency.

          Required action / AC: Allocate real fixture/manifest provisioning and same-case binding before the D2 full gate, or explicitly schedule those real-binding tests into D5 while retaining the complete D1 contract gate and all cumulative D5 obligations. State per-project/category/test IDs at both boundaries; no skips or silent exclusion.

          N10 ADD-AC [medium]: D1/D5 SnapshotObservationHistory_MatchesLoadedBoundary. Source: docs/plans/PLAN-NATIVERESTORE-001.md:90.

          Evidence: obs-emitter.c:23-31,49-63 retains process-local cycle_index and ba_low_run. Snapshot loading restores VICE, not those wrapper fields. A writing run and a resumed run have different prelude lengths, and a resumed BA-low/stolen prefix can require history absent from the prelude.

          Assessment: Ordered VICE_SNAP records solve event identity but do not by themselves align process-relative half-cycle indices or prove correct derived AEC at the first restored cycles. This is an added observable AC, not an observed native failure.

          Required action / AC: D1 mock and D5 real cases must compare the entire approved post-checkpoint window from its first half-cycle, preserving raw process indices/provenance, with correct AEC for a BA-low/stolen first access and unequal/reused prelude clocks. Reject a shifted window, discarded warmup prefix and stale observational history; identify the checkpoint clock/marker used for each origin.


          N11 ADD-AC [medium]: D0/D2 NativeLoadAudit_FailsClosedAndStaysOffHotPath. Source: docs/plans/PLAN-NATIVERESTORE-001.md:80.

          Evidence: The combined resolver is installed only by ViceNative static initialization (:29-33), while ViceNativeXvic has a separate availability/create path (:31-48,141-153). Returning zero also permits runtime fallback probing. The plan names a failing-path control without specifying failed-load versus failed-log behavior.

          Assessment: The detector must cover each process initialization order and be unable to turn an unwritable or absent log into a zero-load pass. The env-gated diagnostic must not introduce per-cycle allocations or alter native step behavior.

          Required action / AC: Use fresh-process controls with xvic first, x64 first, alias/fallback and failing loads; require the audit to observe every attempt. An unwritable log or missing retained gate log fails the gate. With the variable unset and during repeated step calls, prove no logging work/allocation and unchanged deterministic behavior. Keep positive controls in separate logs.

          N13 ADD-AC [medium]: D1/D5 ResumeBudget_HandlesLowClockAndInstructionOvershoot. Source: docs/plans/PLAN-NATIVERESTORE-001.md:79.

          Evidence: Both c64.c:1070 and vic20.c:951 initialize autostart with 3 seconds. autostart.c:1434-1436,1528-1537 delays loading; the CPU limit is checked before autostart_advance and only after maincpu_clk > limit. A 100000-clock fixture plus a short window can terminate in the prelude, and instruction termination can overshoot the last required sample.

          Assessment: The new budget rule rejects that outcome but does not pin a low-clock positive case, the comparison interval length, or how retained tail cycles relate to the required window. A large legacy clock alone does not exercise this boundary.

          Required action / AC: For each required fixture/host, record the prelude limit evidence, exact positive continuation interval, overflow rejection, permit count/protocol and final observation window. Add correct low-clock and restored-clock-above-budget cases plus one-instruction overshoot, prelude-only, overflow and truncated-baseline negative controls. Every required sample remains checked; retained tail evidence is explicitly outside the compared window, never silently dropped.

          Structured what-if and chained-rule audit:

          - Host checkpoint clocks differ: R12 requires both hosts equal before freezing and rejects any later mismatch. It must not silently retarget literal 100000; F05.

          - Pending IRQ/NMI before checkpoint: active 6510dtvcore runs the interrupt sequence before its trap. Trigger and callback clocks may differ; re-arming at the callback clock may defer the trap another instruction; F05.

          - Checkpoint requested within a stolen run: the existing stolen-cycle wrap exposes every clock, but the trap waits for a later dispatch boundary. Preserve the requested cycle or stop under R12, never label it the requested interior snapshot; F05.

          - First resumed access is stolen: N02 permits idle/stolen prefix and requires first CPU access at PC. N10 additionally checks derived AEC/history from the first sample and marker-relative window origin.

          - Shim is reached only through a data provider: R6/N06 must tag its test method transitively while retaining the pure managed fake. N11 audits fresh-process initialization and fallback paths.

          - Load log cannot be written: retained-empty-log evidence is mandatory; N11 makes this a named failing gate and forbids treating no file as zero attempts.

          - The twenty known-red set changes: A05 fails equality for an improvement, new failure or equal-size swap. Retain IDs/TRX and obtain a disposition, never silently update the baseline.

          - Windows autocrlf checkout and interrupted restore: A04 uses Git-clean status for already restored bytes, preserves raw copies, resumes untouched paths and rejects unexpected/staged drift.

          - Crash after first host restore or after both: A03 stops before E, retains originals/evidence and pinned restored source, with no automatic reapply or cleanup.

          - Full CPU load succeeds but a later module fails: ordered resume then load,-1 and reset is rejected; no full-load claim from CPU registers alone, N02.

          - Small restored clock versus autostart prelude, or instruction limit overshoot: N13 requires both a successful full interval and explicit rejected boundaries.

          - Fresh D2 run before D5 fixture generation: N08 requires a closed test-binding/prerequisite allocation without stale artifacts, new skips or a hidden filter.

          - D3 combined with the task-4 Phase D exit: N09 exposes the early-done/deadlock alternatives and moves the done write to step 12.

This is a primary-persisted review, not an attribution of historical queued records as persisted. No commit or push.

            Structured what-if and chained-rule audit:

            - Host checkpoint clocks differ: R12 requires both hosts equal before freezing and rejects any later mismatch. It must not silently retarget literal 100000; F05.

            - Pending IRQ/NMI before checkpoint: active 6510dtvcore runs the interrupt sequence before its trap. Trigger and callback clocks may differ; re-arming at the callback clock may defer the trap another instruction; F05.

            - Checkpoint requested within a stolen run: the existing stolen-cycle wrap exposes every clock, but the trap waits for a later dispatch boundary. Preserve the requested cycle or stop under R12, never label it the requested interior snapshot; F05.

            - First resumed access is stolen: N02 permits idle/stolen prefix and requires first CPU access at PC. N10 additionally checks derived AEC/history from the first sample and marker-relative window origin.

            - Shim is reached only through a data provider: R6/N06 must tag its test method transitively while retaining the pure managed fake. N11 audits fresh-process initialization and fallback paths.

            - Load log cannot be written: retained-empty-log evidence is mandatory; N11 makes this a named failing gate and forbids treating no file as zero attempts.

            - The twenty known-red set changes: A05 fails equality for an improvement, new failure or equal-size swap. Retain IDs/TRX and obtain a disposition, never silently update the baseline.

            - Windows autocrlf checkout and interrupted restore: A04 uses Git-clean status for already restored bytes, preserves raw copies, resumes untouched paths and rejects unexpected/staged drift.

            - Crash after first host restore or after both: A03 stops before E, retains originals/evidence and pinned restored source, with no automatic reapply or cleanup.

            - Full CPU load succeeds but a later module fails: ordered resume then load,-1 and reset is rejected; no full-load claim from CPU registers alone, N02.

            - Small restored clock versus autostart prelude, or instruction limit overshoot: N13 requires both a successful full interval and explicit rejected boundaries.

            - Fresh D2 run before D5 fixture generation: N08 requires a closed test-binding/prerequisite allocation without stale artifacts, new skips or a hidden filter.

            - D3 combined with the task-4 Phase D exit: N09 exposes the early-done/deadlock alternatives and moves the done write to step 12.

          N03 UNKNOWN [high]: R10 does not establish the broader reader-safety claim. Source: docs/plans/PLAN-NATIVERESTORE-001.md:56.

          Evidence: Pinned drive-snapshot.c line 315 leaves rotation_table_ptr uninitialized; absent/TDE-off units bypass its assignment at 394, but line 433 unconditionally passes the array to rotation_table_set. rotation.c lines 189-203 reads each entry at 194 regardless of DriveType, then overwrites speed_zone at 203. Type NONE protects the three clock assignments, not this source read.

          N04 ADD-AC [medium]: D1/D5 SnapshotObserver_PreservesArchitecturalRegistersAndResult. Source: docs/plans/PLAN-NATIVERESTORE-001.md:57.

          Evidence: Pinned mos6510.h lines 116-136 splits N and Z from raw p and recomposes architectural P through MOS6510_REGS_GET_STATUS. The required fixture has P=A0. Successful read populates the globals on both machines, and the call is cross-object; the proposed seam is feasible.

          Assessment: An explicit status-byte and forwarding control prevents an otherwise plausible raw-field implementation from losing flags. This strengthens the chosen R11 B design without rejecting it.

          Required action / acceptance outcome: D1 mock tests must reconstruct architectural P, cover N/Z/unused-bit combinations, wide clock and PC, call the real reader exactly once, preserve return/state, emit no resume on read failure and handle stderr write/flush failure. D5 binds these tests and no-snapshot gate/PNG equivalence to both hosts.


          N05 ADD-AC [medium]: D1/D5 DrivePrecondition_PreservesApprovedContextAndRejectsMalformedCounts. Source: docs/plans/PLAN-NATIVERESTORE-001.md:56.

          Evidence: drive-resources.c set_drive_type detaches images and sets unit type/bus state; drive_disable changes enabled/port state. iecbus.c lines 493-547 makes bus routing depend on resource state. With TDE off, drive-type bit alone does not select true-drive routing, but resource changes still occur. A TDE-on module with has_drives=0 also bypasses per-drive initialization.

          Assessment: The approved precondition has effects beyond bypassing a copy. Its permitted workload/context and invalid DRIVE payload behavior should have explicit observable outcomes rather than an assumed universal equivalence.

          Required action / acceptance outcome: D1 rejects truncated/invalid has_tde/has_drives payloads before launch and proves required NONE flags cannot be overridden. D5 records effective resources/media and demonstrates the approved workload continuation and media context under the precondition on both hosts, or reports a blocker requiring an explicit requirement disposition.

          Review of bus-observer.c:

          1. Your three points hold for a single row layout. Watch rows delegate to the patched rows, so each access ends in exactly one trampoline.

          2. HAZARD before S1b (x64sc):
             - dummy_read_row and dummy_write_row call saved_read[page] and saved_write[page], the originals of the row captured at the last mem_initialize_memory.
             - After a $01 bank switch, mem_update_tab_ptrs re-aliases the dummy pointers to the new config row. The next vice_sharp_bus_cycle repoint_dummy() points them back at the wrapper rows, whose handlers are still the old config's.
             - So dummy cycles then read and write through the old config's handlers, for example RAM where I/O is mapped. Side-effecting dummy reads such as the CIA ICR then change emulation, not just observation.
             - S1b's per-config saved originals fix this; until then, please make sure S1 is never linked into x64sc.
          3. Noise: vice_sharp_bus_cycle emits "foreign read" whenever _mem_read_tab_ptr != known_read_row.
             - With watch flag 1 the real pointer legitimately points at the delegating watch rows (your header lines 153 to 155), so every cycle reports foreign although nothing is foreign.
             - Suggest admitting the watch row as a known delegate or giving it its own kind, so gates that fail on "foreign" stay usable with watchpoints on.
          4. Minor: the translate detail prints *base != NULL after setting *base = NULL, so it is always 0. Logging the real base before the override would carry information.

          <cross-session-message from="bridge:session_01WWp9NxZCvcix3H2PGBFNjR" from-name="LEG-VICE#" from-mode="prompting">

          From LEG-VICE#, 2026-10-20T07:53: on point (3), there is a way to learn the watch row address without touching monitor state. Both ways below only read pointers VICE has already set. Line numbers are from the materialized native/vice tree (read only).


          Facts:

          - vic20mem.c 536 to 556 and c64memsc.c 166 to 192 behave the same way. For any nonzero flag the real pointers are set to the single static watch row: _mem_read_tab_watch on VIC-20, mem_read_tab_watch on x64sc. Flag 1 sets the dummy pointers to the nowatch or config row; flag 2 sets them to the watch row too.

          - The watch row is one 0x101 row on both machines, not one per config, so once learned it stays valid.

          - vic20mem.c 523: mem_initialize_memory ends with an intra-object mem_toggle_watchpoints(watchpoints_active, NULL).

          - The monitor reaches mem_toggle_watchpoints only through maincpu_monitor_interface->toggle_watchpoints_func. That address is taken in maincpu.c 395 (VIC-20) and mainc64cpu.c 622 (x64sc), a cross-object reference that --wrap redirects.


          Way (a), in __wrap_mem_initialize_memory after the real call:

          - If _mem_read_tab_ptr != _mem_read_tab_ptr_dummy, then watch flag is 1 and _mem_read_tab_ptr is the watch row. Record it, and do the same for the write pointer.

          - Flag 2 already makes the dummy pointer the watch row, which you patch.

          - Flag 0 means watch rows are not in use.


          Way (b), add --wrap=mem_toggle_watchpoints:

          - In __wrap_mem_toggle_watchpoints(flag, ctx), call __real first. Then, if flag != 0, record _mem_read_tab_ptr and _mem_write_tab_ptr as the watch rows.

          - This catches every monitor toggle at runtime. Because of the cross-object pointer assignment above, the monitor path is wrapped. The one intra-object call at vic20mem.c 523 is covered by (a), since it runs inside the wrapped mem_initialize_memory.

          - One caveat: --wrap still needs mem_toggle_watchpoints to be defined in a different object from maincpu.c and mainc64cpu.c, which it is (vic20mem.o, c64memsc.o).


          With the watch row known, vice_sharp_bus_cycle can treat _mem_read_tab_ptr == watch row as an allowed delegate rather than "foreign", so a real foreign row still reports.


          Mocks I would add first:

          - flag 1 after a rebuild gives no "foreign" events;

          - a monitor toggle to 1 mid-run gives no "foreign" events;

          - a row that is neither known nor the watch row still gives "foreign".


          Please message me when the S1b wrapper commit is pushed. I will then run --filter BusObserver (expect 50) and the full project (188), and check that bus-probe-rows is compiled beside bus-probe.

          - The fixtures import msvcrt.dll.

          - My probe, compiled inside MSYS2 MINGW64, with stderr dup2'd onto a pipe whose read end is closed:
            - fprintf returns 23, then 17; ferror stays 0; fflush returns -1.
            - With setvbuf(stderr, _IONBF) first: fprintf still returns 23, and ferror is 32.
          - So on msvcrt, fprintf < 0 alone never detects the broken pipe.

          - The generated configure line 3490 sets ac_aux_dir_candidates="${srcdir}${PATH_SEPARATOR}${srcdir}/..${PATH_SEPARATOR}${srcdir}/../..", and lines 3500 to 3502 split it on IFS=$PATH_SEPARATOR, which is ":" under MSYS2.

          - The script invokes configure as "F:/GitHub/.../work-windows/vice/configure", so srcdir=F:/... and the split yields "F" and "/GitHub/...".

          - Reproduced in MSYS2 MINGW64 with that exact loop:
            - srcdir=F:/...: candidates [F] and [/GitHub/.../vice], both "config.guess absent";
            - srcdir=$(cygpath -u F:/...)=/f/GitHub/.../vice: first candidate "config.guess found".
          - The same colon would also break make: the generated Makefiles carry srcdir and VPATH, and ":" is special in both.


          Suggested fix, test first, your call:

          - Hand configure its path in MSYS POSIX form, /f/GitHub/.../work-windows/vice/configure, via cygpath -u in the shell or an F:/x to /f/x conversion. That still uses forward slashes, per the lab rule.

          - gcc arguments can stay C:/... (native gcc understands them).

          - Refuse, before running configure, any configure path that contains ":".


          The build-windows folder now holds a config.log from the failed run. Configure can be rerun over it; nothing needs cleaning. I will rerun Configure, then Make, Link and Inventory, as soon as you push.


          S3 notes received: +logcolorize on non-terminal stdout, -seed in every run, and the STX $01 kind-missing fix. I will apply them in the Windows S3 and S4c runs after S2b.

          - Cause: two makes built the same directory at once.
            - soundwmm.c was compiled twice, and make entered arch/shared/sounddrv twice.
            - In build-windows/src/Makefile, $(archdep_lib) runs "(cd arch/shared && $(MAKE))": all, recursing into SUBDIRS.
            - $(sounddrv_lib) concurrently runs "(cd arch/shared/sounddrv && $(MAKE))".
            - Under -j both run; the first make moves the .Tpo and the second make's mv fails. The Windows-only sound sources (soundwmm, sounddx) make the timing window wider here.
          - I scanned all 89 lib recipes. Exactly two parents recurse ("all") over another lib's directory:
            - archdep_lib (arch/shared) over hwsiddrv, mididrv, socketdrv and sounddrv;
            - core_lib (core) over rtc.

          Suggested fix, test first, your call: pre-build those parents before the per-program targets, the same way as src/lib. For example: make -C src/lib -jN V=1 && make -C src/arch/shared -jN V=1 && make -C src/core -jN V=1 && make -C src -jN V=1 x64sc.exe xvic.exe. Once those archives exist, their $(X_lib) recipes have no prerequisites and do not run again, so nothing recurses twice. Linux has the same race; it is only timing-dependent there.


          Evidence: receipts-windows/make.log, with an identical copy kept as make-attempt1-race.log because a rerun overwrites make.log.

          - Full suite, unfiltered Release: OracleContract 143/143, VsfCompatibility 199/199, NativeClockProbe 153/153; 0 failed, 0 skipped.

          - Inventory unchanged (C7848A24).

          - Receipt: docs/receipts/lockstep/20261019T200626-phase-c-step2-windows/ (TRX, compiler logs, raw hex observations in c-evidence/).

          - Self-assessment: accuracy 98, completeness 93, so not a pass.


          For the Linux binding, three things measured on Windows that will likely differ:

          1. Broken pipe. Windows returns 65: the MSVCRT buffers the stderr write, so fflush reports the failure. On Linux, writing to a closed pipe raises SIGPIPE and kills the process before the gate can report 64 or 65. The broken-stderr fixture accepts 64 or 65 and requires no NATIVE_AFTER line, so a death by signal fails it. That is a real Linux finding; please report it rather than change the fixture. (My first expectation of 64 was my own inference; it is corrected and disclosed in the receipt.)

          2. Stdout retention. On Windows, native stdout written before _Exit is retained. glibc's _Exit does not flush stdio, so Linux likely loses it; that would explain F-04's seven empty logs. The continue-then-quit case measures stdout without asserting it, and writes it to real-stdio-observations.log.

          3. Line endings. Windows text mode emits CRLF, which the fixtures expect only on Windows (Nl = "\r\n"); Linux expects LF. Consequence for step 3: the VICE_OBS contract treats CRLF as a framing fault, so the Windows wrapper must set binary mode. That is noted as a step 3 item.


          Next on my side: Phase C step 3. Extend vic_cycle and the clock-gate wrapper to emit VICE_OBS for xvic, then x64sc, by link-time wrapping from artifacts/immutable-vice. I'll read the existing wrapper and the materialization first and report any field I can't observe as a named blocker.

          > D9, the answer you never got.           > WSMan remote shells own a Windows job object; child processes are terminated when the shell is torn down, which is why a Start-Process launch “succeeds” and then vanishes. The usual escapes are a scheduled task, a service, or Win32_Process.Create through WMI, and the last one needs DCOM launch rights the lab-wsman account does not have. Running synchronously is the honest fallback as long as the remote script writes its own receipts incrementally, which this one does.


          Instead of managing Legion2 from Omarchy, start an agent with claude remote on Legion2 to do the Windows tasks.
